## UDP round trip above the raw floor (µs)

The raw floor is the floor client's round trip in the same state (md-dpdk/harness/src/bin/floor.rs: rtt's pacing, warm-up, clock, CPUs and 64-byte datagrams, with no Aeron driver or client: kernel UDP sockets for the kernel's modes, the DPDK transport's bindings alone for DPDK's). Each Aeron mode's round trip less its floor's, rep by rep where both ran: the median across reps, the range in brackets; what Aeron's driver and client add. A max no higher than the floor's lies beneath Aeron. dpdk-raw is set against the pure floor (purefloor.c: DPDK alone, a 5-tuple-swapping reflector at the pong, the port set up as the transport's): what ergon-md-dpdk's packet path adds; a max no higher than the pure floor's lies beneath the transport. The pure floor sends and receives on each host's receiver port alone, dpdk-raw sends on the sender port. A mode with overrides is set against the floor with the same overrides where that ran, else the bare floor.

## Same-host hairpin against the cross-host round trip (µs)

The hairpin (@HAIRPIN=1) is the same mode's round trip on host a alone, from its sender NIC to its receiver NIC and back, never to host b; the cross-host one is host a to host b. Where AWS turns the hairpin round is not documented: a hairpin p50 well under the cross-host one puts it in host a's own card. Medians across reps, the range in brackets. Maxima in the hairpin as large as across the hosts come from host a (its card, the hypervisor, the guest); maxima across the hosts alone come from the fabric or host b.

| state, transport                                 | reps hairpin | reps cross-host | p50 hairpin         | p50 cross-host      | p99.99 hairpin      | p99.99 cross-host   | max hairpin           | max cross-host         |
| ------------------------------------------------ | ------------ | --------------- | ------------------- | ------------------- | ------------------- | ------------------- | --------------------- | ---------------------- |
| isolated, DPDK packet path, no Aeron (raw floor) | 3            | 3               | 12.38 (12.38–12.39) | 24.73 (24.57–24.80) | 27.63 (27.61–29.30) | 45.53 (42.59–46.08) | 61.63 (38.78–75.65)   | 88.06 (85.57–91.20)    |
| isolated, kernel UDP, no Aeron (raw floor)       | 3            | 3               | 16.09 (16.04–16.09) | 28.32 (28.25–28.46) | 32.38 (32.32–33.60) | 50.46 (50.34–50.56) | 116.16 (85.44–148.22) | 139.52 (130.81–142.21) |

## Sparse round trips by gap (µs)

A paced mode (@RTT_GAP_US) sends one ping every gap on a fixed schedule, the clients spinning in between, so only the network goes quiet; `none` is the same mode unpaced, each ping as the last one's echo is back. Aeron's heartbeats are 100 ms apart on an idle channel. Medians across reps, the range in brackets; a rep is 20 to 60 s (modes.sh rtt_plan), so a sparse one holds few round trips: p99.99 only from 10,000 a rep, and at a few hundred p99 is near the max. A p50 or p99 that grows with the gap is the path slowing for a flow that goes quiet.

## Aeron's agents and flow control by run (information, never loss)

From Aeron's own counters on each host, read with the loss counters around every run (vm.sh loss). An agent's longest duty cycle counts from its driver's start (one driver a mode and rep), so a run's is the longest up to its end: the highest over reps here. Its cycles over the threshold vm.sh sets (AERON_DRIVER_{CONDUCTOR,SENDER,RECEIVER}_CYCLE_THRESHOLD), the sender's flow-control limits (back-pressure events), the status messages sent and received and the flow control under and over runs are each run's growth, summed over reps. - where a host's rows lack one. None of them is loss.
